GAJENDRA THAKUR / TEN SOURCE EDITIONS

Diwan-e-Videha Khand 2

Gajendra Thakur · Ghazals with musical forms · 1200 PDF pages

The book and its concerns

Khand 2 now stands as the full second volume beside Khand 1: it opens with an alphabetical Devanagari index for ghazals 592–1181, then three formal essays on ghazal structure, metre and minimum qualifications for calling a poem a ghazal. The main body continues the diwan through 590 numbered ghazals.

Its distinctive feature is the paired musical-form companion for each ghazal. The page therefore treats every ghazal as a double unit: literary text plus performance-facing musical note. Khand 2 is not only a numerical continuation of Khand 1; it is a more navigable and more performative volume, with index, scansion discipline and musical presentation held together.

INDIVIDUAL READINGS

594 unit discussions

These discussions include endings where the source unit has one. Each entry separates narrative or lyric gist from editorial interpretation and cites its place in the supplied Word edition. English headings are descriptive translations.

Reference locations count non-empty Word paragraphs in document order, including table text; these are not printed page numbers. These are AI-assisted editorial discussions; no named human review is recorded.

This opening unit records the book’s alphabetical Devanagari index for ghazals 592–1181. Its presence changes Khand 2 from a simple continuation volume into a navigable diwan: title, number and alphabetic access are made part of the reading apparatus.

The gist is bibliographic rather than lyrical. Readers can approach the volume by sequence or by Devanagari title-order, a useful feature for citation, teaching and returning to a particular ghazal after listening or reading.

Ghazal 614, “नेहक गान बचल अछि,” is presented in the full Khand 2 as a verified ghazal unit rather than an isolated lyric. Its entry carries metre, rhyme-return, refrain and source-position information, so the poem is read through both meaning and formal discipline.

The paired musical-form companion is integral here. It supplies mood, tempo or performance guidance beside the text, making Khand 2 different from Khand 1: each ghazal becomes a literary and musical object, available for close reading and for sung or sounded realization.

Ghazal 720, “साँच लेल कोन माप अछि,” is presented in the full Khand 2 as a verified ghazal unit rather than an isolated lyric. Its entry carries metre, rhyme-return, refrain and source-position information, so the poem is read through both meaning and formal discipline.

The paired musical-form companion is integral here. It supplies mood, tempo or performance guidance beside the text, making Khand 2 different from Khand 1: each ghazal becomes a literary and musical object, available for close reading and for sung or sounded realization.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड २, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Khand_2_592-1181_FINAL_2COL_ANUKRAMANIKA.docx, non-empty paragraphs 3487–3515. Edition details.

Ghazal 814, “मेह राति पड़ै छै, नेह छप्पर बजैए,” is presented in the full Khand 2 as a verified ghazal unit rather than an isolated lyric. Its entry carries metre, rhyme-return, refrain and source-position information, so the poem is read through both meaning and formal discipline.

The paired musical-form companion is integral here. It supplies mood, tempo or performance guidance beside the text, making Khand 2 different from Khand 1: each ghazal becomes a literary and musical object, available for close reading and for sung or sounded realization.

Ghazal 860, “बाढ़ नीर उतरै छै, खेत माटि दिसै,” is presented in the full Khand 2 as a verified ghazal unit rather than an isolated lyric. Its entry carries metre, rhyme-return, refrain and source-position information, so the poem is read through both meaning and formal discipline.

The paired musical-form companion is integral here. It supplies mood, tempo or performance guidance beside the text, making Khand 2 different from Khand 1: each ghazal becomes a literary and musical object, available for close reading and for sung or sounded realization.

Ghazal 1024, “झूला आमगाछ हिलै, उमंग पवन संग डोलैत रहैए,” is presented in the full Khand 2 as a verified ghazal unit rather than an isolated lyric. Its entry carries metre, rhyme-return, refrain and source-position information, so the poem is read through both meaning and formal discipline.

The paired musical-form companion is integral here. It supplies mood, tempo or performance guidance beside the text, making Khand 2 different from Khand 1: each ghazal becomes a literary and musical object, available for close reading and for sung or sounded realization.
